{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JTLPJyPNbSuskwgzLP7BD9","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport { readFileSync } from 'fs';\n2\timport { basename, join, relative } from 'path';\n3\timport { execFileSync } from 'child_process';\n4\t\n5\timport { MODULE_SCHEMAS } from '../../libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-manager.service';\n6\timport { stripComments } from './helpers/ts-source';\n7\t\n8\t/**\n9\t * Tenant provisioning is migration REPLAY: the orchestrator pins\n10\t * `search_path` to `tenant_<uuid>` and runs each tenant-aware service's\n11\t * migration set. That mechanism only redirects UNQUALIFIED DDL. Anything that\n12\t * names its schema explicitly — or re-pins the session to the source schema —\n13\t * escapes it, lands in the source schema, and is STILL recorded in the tenant's\n14\t * ledger as applied. This spec is the gate for that class.\n15\t *\n16\t * ## Why this spec was rewritten (DATA-HIGH-012)\n17\t *\n18\t * The previous detector was a single regex looking for a DDL keyword followed\n19\t * by a `\"<source schema>\".` identifier, plus a filename allowlist and a blanket\n20\t * exclusion for every Baseline. It enumerated one SPELLING of the defect rather\n21\t * than testing the property, and both halves of that leaked:\n22\t *\n23\t *   - MSG-HIGH-077 shipped a per-tenant migration that re-pinned its own source\n24\t *     schema — functionally identical to qualifying every statement in the file\n25\t *     — CITING THIS GUARD in its docblock as evidence of its own correctness.\n26\t *   - DATA-CRITICAL-010 (no new tenant can be provisioned) lived entirely\n27\t *     inside the Baseline exclusion.\n28\t *\n29\t * It now asks the actual question — *does this migration issue DDL against a\n30\t * PER-TENANT table in the source schema, by either spelling?* — using\n31\t * `MODULE_SCHEMAS` as the classifier. That is the same SSoT the entity layer\n32\t * obeys via `entity-schema-declaration`, so the guard cannot drift from it, and\n33\t * the Baselines are now checked like every other migration rather than excused.\n34\t *\n35\t * `scripts/migration/dequalify-tenant-baselines.mjs` reads the same registry to\n36\t * decide what to rewrite. Two consumers of one SSoT, not two SSoTs.\n37\t *\n38\t * ## Fail-closed\n39\t *\n40\t * A qualified identifier the registry cannot explain is a VIOLATION, not a\n41\t * pass. An unregistered per-tenant table is exactly the shape that would\n42\t * otherwise slip through, so \"I don't recognise this\" resolves against the\n43\t * migration and lands in the reviewer allowlist below with a written reason.\n44\t */\n45\t\n46\tconst REPO_ROOT = join(__dirname, '..', '..');\n47\t\n48\t/** Source schema per tenant-aware migration directory. */\n49\tconst TENANT_AWARE_MIGRATION_DIRS: ReadonlyArray<readonly [string, string]> = [\n50\t  ['apps/farm-service/src/database/migrations', 'farm'],\n51\t  ['apps/sensor-service/src/database/migrations', 'sensor'],\n52\t  ['apps/hr-service/src/database/migrations', 'hr'],\n53\t  ['apps/messaging-service/src/migrations', 'messaging'],\n54\t  ['apps/messaging-service/src/database/migrations', 'messaging'],\n55\t  ['apps/alert-engine/src/database/migrations', 'alert'],\n56\t  ['apps/ai-service/src/database/migrations', 'ai'],\n57\t  ['apps/hydroponics-service/src/database/migrations', 'hydroponics'],\n58\t];\n59\t\n60\t/** A DDL verb + object immediately preceding a qualified identifier. */\n61\tconst DDL_LEAD =\n62\t  /\\b(?:CREATE|ALTER|DROP|TRUNCATE)\\s+(?:UNIQUE\\s+)?(?:TABLE|INDEX|TYPE|SEQUENCE|VIEW|MATERIALIZED\\s+VIEW)\\b(?:\\s+IF\\s+(?:NOT\\s+)?EXISTS)?[\\s\\S]{0,220}$/i;\n63\t\n64\t/**\n65\t * Tables that exist in a shipped migration's DDL but in NEITHER\n66\t * `MODULE_SCHEMAS[].tables` nor `[].infrastructureTables`, because a later\n67\t * migration retired them. They cannot be classified and they cannot be edited\n68\t * out of an immutable migration, so they are named here with the migration that\n69\t * retired them. A NEW name appearing in a failure means an entity shipped\n70\t * without its registry entry — register it, do not add it here.\n71\t */\n72\tconst RETIRED_SOURCE_TABLES: ReadonlyMap<string, ReadonlySet<string>> = new Map([\n73\t  // Superseded by the `sensor_metrics` hypertable; the per-tenant copy of that\n74\t  // is created unqualified by 1815000000000.\n75\t  ['sensor', new Set(['sensor_readings'])],\n76\t  // Dropped by 1802100000000-DropTenantAiSettings.\n77\t  ['messaging', new Set(['tenant_ai_settings'])],\n78\t]);\n79\t\n80\t/**\n81\t * Migrations whose source-schema-qualified DDL has been REVIEWED as legitimate.\n82\t * This is a reviewer-gated allowlist in THIS spec, not a self-service docblock\n83\t * marker: ORPHAN-HIGH-408's `CreateAiProposedActions` used the old\n84\t * `TENANT_AWARE_SOURCE_SCHEMA_DDL_OK` docblock marker to schema-qualify a\n85\t * PER-TENANT table, which the replay then never landed in any tenant. A\n86\t * self-service marker bypasses CI silently; an allowlist edit shows up in the\n87\t * PR diff. Adding an entry must be justified in writing.\n88\t */\n89\tconst REVIEWED_SOURCE_SCHEMA_DDL: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set([\n90\t  // Source-only enum reclaim/heal of the 2026-07-07 QualityGrade outage.\n91\t  '1804400000000-DropOrphanQualityGradeEnum.ts',\n92\t  '1804500000000-HealBehindTenantQualityGrade.ts',\n93\t  // Cross-tenant source-schema tables + partition-contract index (not cloned).\n94\t  '1800300000000-SensorV2TenantFkAndLicenseGrant.ts',\n95\t  '1802000000000-AddVfdDeviceModelSeriesPumpTags.ts',\n96\t  '1804000000000-ConsolidateVfdRegisterMappingsToSensorSchema.ts',\n97\t  // SENSOR-CRITICAL-007 Slice 1: nullable edge-binding columns on the per-tenant\n98\t  // vfd_devices table via the SAME source-template + tenant fan-out pattern as\n99\t  // 1802000000000 above (canonical `sensor` copy + every `tenant_*` copy). Not a\n100\t  // per-tenant table wrongly schema-qualified — the fan-out lands it everywhere.\n101\t  '1808000000000-AddVfdDeviceEdgeBinding.ts',\n102\t  // SEC-MEDIUM-083: partial unique index on the per-tenant vfd_change_sets\n103\t  // table — canonical `sensor` copy + tenant fan-out (same pattern as\n104\t  // 1808000000000 above). Per-tenant, not misqualified.\n105\t  '1818000000000-EnforceSingleActiveVfdChangeSet.ts',\n106\t  // SENSOR-HIGH-064: nullable config-ack tracking columns on the per-tenant\n107\t  // edge_devices table via the SAME source-template + tenant fan-out pattern\n108\t  // (canonical `sensor` copy + every `tenant_*` copy).\n109\t  '1809000000000-AddEdgeDeviceConfigAckTracking.ts',\n110\t  // SENSOR-HIGH-083: per-tenant calibration_events table + nullable\n111\t  // calibration_interval_days column on sensor_data_channels, both created in the\n112\t  // canonical `sensor` source schema and fanned out into every `tenant_*` schema\n113\t  // (CREATE TABLE … LIKE INCLUDING ALL for the clone). Per-tenant, not misqualified.\n114\t  '1810000000000-AddCalibrationEventsAndInterval.ts',\n115\t  '1803000000000-HashProvisioningSecretsAtRest.ts',\n116\t  '1800500000000-EnsureMessagingPartitionContract.ts',\n117\t  // ORPHAN-HIGH-408: immutable historical BUG — `ai_proposed_actions` is a\n118\t  // per-tenant table wrongly schema-qualified. Retained only because migrations\n119\t  // are immutable; HEALED by 1803100000000-HealAiProposedActionsUnqualified.\n120\t  // DO NOT copy this pattern: per-tenant tables must be UNQUALIFIED.\n121\t  '1803000000000-CreateAiProposedActions.ts',\n122\t]);\n123\t\n124\tinterface Registry {\n125\t  readonly perTenant: ReadonlySet<string>;\n126\t  readonly infrastructure: ReadonlySet<string>;\n127\t  readonly retired: ReadonlySet<string>;\n128\t}\n129\t\n130\tfunction registryFor(schema: string): Registry {\n131\t  const entry = MODULE_SCHEMAS.find((m) => m.sourceSchema === schema);\n132\t  if (entry === undefined) {\n133\t    throw new Error(`MODULE_SCHEMAS has no entry for source schema \"${schema}\"`);\n134\t  }\n135\t  return {\n136\t    perTenant: new Set([...entry.tables, ...(entry.referenceDataTables ?? [])]),\n137\t    infrastructure: new Set(entry.infrastructureTables ?? []),\n138\t    retired: RETIRED_SOURCE_TABLES.get(schema) ?? new Set<string>(),\n139\t  };\n140\t}\n141\t\n142\t/**\n143\t * `CREATE [UNIQUE] INDEX \"name\" ON \"schema\".\"table\"` — an index inherits its\n144\t * table's classification, and `DROP INDEX \"schema\".\"name\"` names no table, so\n145\t * the map has to be built from the create side first.\n146\t */\n147\tfunction indexOwners(source: string, schema: string): Map<string, string> {\n148\t  const owners = new Map<string, string>();\n149\t  const rx = new RegExp(\n150\t    `CREATE\\\\s+(?:UNIQUE\\\\s+)?INDEX\\\\s+(?:IF\\\\s+NOT\\\\s+EXISTS\\\\s+)?\"([^\"]+)\"\\\\s+ON\\\\s+(?:\"${schema}\"\\\\.)?\"([^\"]+)\"`,\n151\t    'gi',\n152\t  );\n153\t  for (const match of source.matchAll(rx)) {\n154\t    if (match[1] !== undefined && match[2] !== undefined) owners.set(match[1], match[2]);\n155\t  }\n156\t  return owners;\n157\t}\n158\t\n159\t/**\n160\t * Enum type names from their own definitions rather than from a naming\n161\t * convention — farm's `equipment_category` is an enum and carries no `_enum`\n162\t * suffix. Enums are DELIBERATELY left qualified: existing tenants SHARE the\n163\t * source-schema type (`CREATE TABLE … LIKE INCLUDING ALL` shares a type, it does\n164\t * not clone it), so de-qualifying would split the fleet in two.\n165\t */\n166\tfunction enumTypes(source: string, schema: string): Set<string> {\n167\t  const names = new Set<string>();\n168\t  const rx = new RegExp(`CREATE\\\\s+TYPE\\\\s+\"${schema}\"\\\\.\"([^\"]+)\"\\\\s+AS\\\\s+ENUM`, 'gi');\n169\t  for (const match of source.matchAll(rx)) {\n170\t    if (match[1] !== undefined) names.add(match[1]);\n171\t  }\n172\t  return names;\n173\t}\n174\t\n175\ttype Verdict = 'per-tenant' | 'cross-tenant' | 'enum' | 'retired' | 'unclassified';\n176\t\n177\tfunction classify(\n178\t  identifier: string,\n179\t  registry: Registry,\n180\t  indexes: Map<string, string>,\n181\t  enums: Set<string>,\n182\t): Verdict {\n183\t  if (enums.has(identifier)) return 'enum';\n184\t  if (registry.perTenant.has(identifier)) return 'per-tenant';\n185\t  if (registry.infrastructure.has(identifier)) return 'cross-tenant';\n186\t  if (registry.retired.has(identifier)) return 'retired';\n187\t  const owner = indexes.get(identifier);\n188\t  if (owner !== undefined && owner !== identifier) {\n189\t    return classify(owner, registry, indexes, enums);\n190\t  }\n191\t  return 'unclassified';\n192\t}\n193\t\n194\tinterface Offence {\n195\t  readonly file: string;\n196\t  readonly reason: string;\n197\t}\n198\t\n199\t/** Qualified DDL naming something that must not live in the source schema. */\n200\tfunction qualifiedDdlOffences(file: string, source: string, schema: string): Offence[] {\n201\t  const indexes = indexOwners(source, schema);\n202\t  const enums = enumTypes(source, schema);\n203\t  const registry = registryFor(schema);\n204\t  const offences: Offence[] = [];\n205\t  const seen = new Set<string>();\n206\t\n207\t  const rx = new RegExp(`\"${schema}\"\\\\.\"([^\"]+)\"`, 'g');\n208\t  for (const match of source.matchAll(rx)) {\n209\t    const identifier = match[1];\n210\t    if (identifier === undefined) continue;\n211\t    // Only DDL counts. A qualified name inside a DML statement, a\n212\t    // `to_regclass()` probe or a RAISE message is not a routing decision.\n213\t    if (!DDL_LEAD.test(source.slice(0, match.index))) continue;\n214\t\n215\t    const verdict = classify(identifier, registry, indexes, enums);\n216\t    if (verdict === 'cross-tenant' || verdict === 'enum' || verdict === 'retired') continue;\n217\t    if (seen.has(identifier)) continue;\n218\t    seen.add(identifier);\n219\t\n220\t    offences.push({\n221\t      file,\n222\t      reason:\n223\t        verdict === 'per-tenant'\n224\t          ? `DDL on \"${schema}\".\"${identifier}\" — a PER-TENANT table (MODULE_SCHEMAS.${schema}.tables). ` +\n225\t            `The replay's search_path cannot redirect a qualified name, so every tenant pass writes to ` +\n226\t            `the source schema and the tenant ledger still records it as applied. Drop the \"${schema}\". prefix.`\n227\t          : `DDL on \"${schema}\".\"${identifier}\" — not in MODULE_SCHEMAS.${schema}.tables OR ` +\n228\t            `.infrastructureTables, so this guard cannot tell whether it belongs in a tenant schema. ` +\n229\t            `Register the table, or add the migration to REVIEWED_SOURCE_SCHEMA_DDL with a written reason.`,\n230\t    });\n231\t  }\n232\t  return offences;\n233\t}\n234\t\n235\t/**\n236\t * `pinSearchPath(qr, '<own source schema>')` in a migration that is NOT\n237\t * `@SourceOnlyMigration` — the second spelling of the same defect. The helper\n238\t * (`libs/backend-common/src/database/base-migration.ts`) issues\n239\t * `SET search_path TO \"<its literal argument>\", public` and has no tenant\n240\t * awareness, so it OVERRIDES the runner's tenant pin for the rest of the\n241\t * migration. Legitimate on a source-only migration, where the tenant pass skips\n242\t * the file entirely; a silent tenant-data hole anywhere else.\n243\t */\n244\tfunction sourcePinOffences(file: string, source: string, schema: string): Offence[] {\n245\t  if (/@SourceOnlyMigration\\s*\\(/.test(source)) return [];\n246\t  const rx = new RegExp(\n247\t    `\\\\bpinSearchPath\\\\s*\\\\(\\\\s*[A-Za-z_$][\\\\w$]*\\\\s*,\\\\s*['\"\\`]${schema}['\"\\`]`,\n248\t    'g',\n249\t  );\n250\t  if (!rx.test(source)) return [];\n251\t  return [\n252\t    {\n253\t      file,\n254\t      reason:\n255\t        `pinSearchPath(queryRunner, '${schema}') in a migration that is not @SourceOnlyMigration. ` +\n256\t        `It pins its literal argument, so a tenant pass re-pins the SOURCE schema, the DDL no-ops ` +\n257\t        `against rows that already exist there, and the tenant ledger records a successful apply ` +\n258\t        `anyway (MSG-HIGH-077). Either drop the pin and follow the runner's, or mark the migration ` +\n259\t        `@SourceOnlyMigration if the table really is cross-tenant.`,\n260\t    },\n261\t  ];\n262\t}\n263\t\n264\tfunction migrationFiles(): Array<readonly [string, string]> {\n265\t  const args = ['ls-files', ...TENANT_AWARE_MIGRATION_DIRS.map(([dir]) => `${dir}/[0-9]*.ts`)];\n266\t  const files = execFileSync('git', args, { cwd: REPO_ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' })\n267\t    .split('\\n')\n268\t    .map((line) => line.trim())\n269\t    .filter(Boolean);\n270\t\n271\t  return files.map((file) => {\n272\t    const match = TENANT_AWARE_MIGRATION_DIRS.find(([dir]) => file.startsWith(`${dir}/`));\n273\t    if (match === undefined) throw new Error(`no source schema mapped for ${file}`);\n274\t    return [file, match[1]] as const;\n275\t  });\n276\t}\n277\t\n278\tdescribe('tenant-aware migration DDL guard', () => {\n279\t  it('no tenant-aware migration issues source-schema DDL against a per-tenant table', () => {\n280\t    const offences: Offence[] = [];\n281\t    for (const [file, schema] of migrationFiles()) {\n282\t      if (REVIEWED_SOURCE_SCHEMA_DDL.has(basename(file))) continue;\n283\t      const source = stripComments(readFileSync(join(REPO_ROOT, file), 'utf8'));\n284\t      offences.push(...qualifiedDdlOffences(relative(REPO_ROOT, file), source, schema));\n285\t    }\n286\t\n287\t    expect(offences.map((o) => `${o.file}: ${o.reason}`)).toEqual([]);\n288\t  });\n289\t\n290\t  it('no tenant-aware migration re-pins its own source schema unless it is source-only', () => {\n291\t    const offences: Offence[] = [];\n292\t    for (const [file, schema] of migrationFiles()) {\n293\t      const source = stripComments(readFileSync(join(REPO_ROOT, file), 'utf8'));\n294\t      offences.push(...sourcePinOffences(relative(REPO_ROOT, file), source, schema));\n295\t    }\n296\t\n297\t    expect(offences.map((o) => `${o.file}: ${o.reason}`)).toEqual([]);\n298\t  });\n299\t\n300\t  it('every allowlisted migration still exists (no stale exemptions)', () => {\n301\t    const present = new Set(migrationFiles().map(([f]) => basename(f)));\n302\t    const stale = [...REVIEWED_SOURCE_SCHEMA_DDL].filter((name) => !present.has(name));\n303\t    expect(stale).toEqual([]);\n304\t  });\n305\t\n306\t  it('every retired-table entry names a table the registry really does not carry', () => {\n307\t    const stale: string[] = [];\n308\t    for (const [schema, tables] of RETIRED_SOURCE_TABLES) {\n309\t      const registry = registryFor(schema);\n310\t      for (const table of tables) {\n311\t        if (registry.perTenant.has(table) || registry.infrastructure.has(table)) {\n312\t          stale.push(`${schema}.${table} is registered again — drop it from RETIRED_SOURCE_TABLES`);\n313\t        }\n314\t      }\n315\t    }\n316\t    expect(stale).toEqual([]);\n317\t  });\n318\t});\n319\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b26701de-de54-4da0-aea9-d7d646f071ab","uuid":"a36e7a88-7b88-4d99-85ce-6a6730d8cc49","timestamp":"2026-10-07T10:44:08.897Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-8ce672d7a9e3/tests/invariants/tenant-aware-migration-ddl-guard.spec.ts","content":"import { readFileSync } from 'fs';\nimport { basename, join, relative } from 'path';\nimport { execFileSync } from 'child_process';\n\nimport { MODULE_SCHEMAS } from '../../libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-manager.service';\nimport { stripComments } from './helpers/ts-source';\n\n/**\n * Tenant provisioning is migration REPLAY: the orchestrator pins\n * `search_path` to `tenant_<uuid>` and runs each tenant-aware service's\n * migration set. That mechanism only redirects UNQUALIFIED DDL. Anything that\n * names its schema explicitly — or re-pins the session to the source schema —\n * escapes it, lands in the source schema, and is STILL recorded in the tenant's\n * ledger as applied. This spec is the gate for that class.\n *\n * ## Why this spec was rewritten (DATA-HIGH-012)\n *\n * The previous detector was a single regex looking for a DDL keyword followed\n * by a `\"<source schema>\".` identifier, plus a filename allowlist and a blanket\n * exclusion for every Baseline. It enumerated one SPELLING of the defect rather\n * than testing the property, and both halves of that leaked:\n *\n *   - MSG-HIGH-077 shipped a per-tenant migration that re-pinned its own source\n *     schema — functionally identical to qualifying every statement in the file\n *     — CITING THIS GUARD in its docblock as evidence of its own correctness.\n *   - DATA-CRITICAL-010 (no new tenant can be provisioned) lived entirely\n *     inside the Baseline exclusion.\n *\n * It now asks the actual question — *does this migration issue DDL against a\n * PER-TENANT table in the source schema, by either spelling?* — using\n * `MODULE_SCHEMAS` as the classifier. That is the same SSoT the entity layer\n * obeys via `entity-schema-declaration`, so the guard cannot drift from it, and\n * the Baselines are now checked like every other migration rather than excused.\n *\n * `scripts/migration/dequalify-tenant-baselines.mjs` reads the same registry to\n * decide what to rewrite. Two consumers of one SSoT, not two SSoTs.\n *\n * ## Fail-closed\n *\n * A qualified identifier the registry cannot explain is a VIOLATION, not a\n * pass. An unregistered per-tenant table is exactly the shape that would\n * otherwise slip through, so \"I don't recognise this\" resolves against the\n * migration and lands in the reviewer allowlist below with a written reason.\n */\n\nconst REPO_ROOT = join(__dirname, '..', '..');\n\n/** Source schema per tenant-aware migration directory. */\nconst TENANT_AWARE_MIGRATION_DIRS: ReadonlyArray<readonly [string, string]> = [\n  ['apps/farm-service/src/database/migrations', 'farm'],\n  ['apps/sensor-service/src/database/migrations', 'sensor'],\n  ['apps/hr-service/src/database/migrations', 'hr'],\n  ['apps/messaging-service/src/migrations', 'messaging'],\n  ['apps/messaging-service/src/database/migrations', 'messaging'],\n  ['apps/alert-engine/src/database/migrations', 'alert'],\n  ['apps/ai-service/src/database/migrations', 'ai'],\n  ['apps/hydroponics-service/src/database/migrations', 'hydroponics'],\n];\n\n/** A DDL verb + object immediately preceding a qualified identifier. */\nconst DDL_LEAD =\n  /\\b(?:CREATE|ALTER|DROP|TRUNCATE)\\s+(?:UNIQUE\\s+)?(?:TABLE|INDEX|TYPE|SEQUENCE|VIEW|MATERIALIZED\\s+VIEW)\\b(?:\\s+IF\\s+(?:NOT\\s+)?EXISTS)?[\\s\\S]{0,220}$/i;\n\n/**\n * Tables that exist in a shipped migration's DDL but in NEITHER\n * `MODULE_SCHEMAS[].tables` nor `[].infrastructureTables`, because a later\n * migration retired them. They cannot be classified and they cannot be edited\n * out of an immutable migration, so they are named here with the migration that\n * retired them. A NEW name appearing in a failure means an entity shipped\n * without its registry entry — register it, do not add it here.\n */\nconst RETIRED_SOURCE_TABLES: ReadonlyMap<string, ReadonlySet<string>> = new Map([\n  // Superseded by the `sensor_metrics` hypertable; the per-tenant copy of that\n  // is created unqualified by 1815000000000.\n  ['sensor', new Set(['sensor_readings'])],\n  // Dropped by 1802100000000-DropTenantAiSettings.\n  ['messaging', new Set(['tenant_ai_settings'])],\n]);\n\n/**\n * Migrations whose source-schema-qualified DDL has been REVIEWED as legitimate.\n * This is a reviewer-gated allowlist in THIS spec, not a self-service docblock\n * marker: ORPHAN-HIGH-408's `CreateAiProposedActions` used the old\n * `TENANT_AWARE_SOURCE_SCHEMA_DDL_OK` docblock marker to schema-qualify a\n * PER-TENANT table, which the replay then never landed in any tenant. A\n * self-service marker bypasses CI silently; an allowlist edit shows up in the\n * PR diff. Adding an entry must be justified in writing.\n */\nconst REVIEWED_SOURCE_SCHEMA_DDL: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set([\n  // Source-only enum reclaim/heal of the 2026-07-07 QualityGrade outage.\n  '1804400000000-DropOrphanQualityGradeEnum.ts',\n  '1804500000000-HealBehindTenantQualityGrade.ts',\n  // Cross-tenant source-schema tables + partition-contract index (not cloned).\n  '1800300000000-SensorV2TenantFkAndLicenseGrant.ts',\n  '1802000000000-AddVfdDeviceModelSeriesPumpTags.ts',\n  '1804000000000-ConsolidateVfdRegisterMappingsToSensorSchema.ts',\n  // SENSOR-CRITICAL-007 Slice 1: nullable edge-binding columns on the per-tenant\n  // vfd_devices table via the SAME source-template + tenant fan-out pattern as\n  // 1802000000000 above (canonical `sensor` copy + every `tenant_*` copy). Not a\n  // per-tenant table wrongly schema-qualified — the fan-out lands it everywhere.\n  '1808000000000-AddVfdDeviceEdgeBinding.ts',\n  // SEC-MEDIUM-083: partial unique index on the per-tenant vfd_change_sets\n  // table — canonical `sensor` copy + tenant fan-out (same pattern as\n  // 1808000000000 above). Per-tenant, not misqualified.\n  '1818000000000-EnforceSingleActiveVfdChangeSet.ts',\n  // SENSOR-HIGH-064: nullable config-ack tracking columns on the per-tenant\n  // edge_devices table via the SAME source-template + tenant fan-out pattern\n  // (canonical `sensor` copy + every `tenant_*` copy).\n  '1809000000000-AddEdgeDeviceConfigAckTracking.ts',\n  // SENSOR-HIGH-083: per-tenant calibration_events table + nullable\n  // calibration_interval_days column on sensor_data_channels, both created in the\n  // canonical `sensor` source schema and fanned out into every `tenant_*` schema\n  // (CREATE TABLE … LIKE INCLUDING ALL for the clone). Per-tenant, not misqualified.\n  '1810000000000-AddCalibrationEventsAndInterval.ts',\n  '1803000000000-HashProvisioningSecretsAtRest.ts',\n  '1800500000000-EnsureMessagingPartitionContract.ts',\n  // ORPHAN-HIGH-408: immutable historical BUG — `ai_proposed_actions` is a\n  // per-tenant table wrongly schema-qualified. Retained only because migrations\n  // are immutable; HEALED by 1803100000000-HealAiProposedActionsUnqualified.\n  // DO NOT copy this pattern: per-tenant tables must be UNQUALIFIED.\n  '1803000000000-CreateAiProposedActions.ts',\n]);\n\ninterface Registry {\n  readonly perTenant: ReadonlySet<string>;\n  readonly infrastructure: ReadonlySet<string>;\n  readonly retired: ReadonlySet<string>;\n}\n\nfunction registryFor(schema: string): Registry {\n  const entry = MODULE_SCHEMAS.find((m) => m.sourceSchema === schema);\n  if (entry === undefined) {\n    throw new Error(`MODULE_SCHEMAS has no entry for source schema \"${schema}\"`);\n  }\n  return {\n    perTenant: new Set([...entry.tables, ...(entry.referenceDataTables ?? [])]),\n    infrastructure: new Set(entry.infrastructureTables ?? []),\n    retired: RETIRED_SOURCE_TABLES.get(schema) ?? new Set<string>(),\n  };\n}\n\n/**\n * `CREATE [UNIQUE] INDEX \"name\" ON \"schema\".\"table\"` — an index inherits its\n * table's classification, and `DROP INDEX \"schema\".\"name\"` names no table, so\n * the map has to be built from the create side first.\n */\nfunction indexOwners(source: string, schema: string): Map<string, string> {\n  const owners = new Map<string, string>();\n  const rx = new RegExp(\n    `CREATE\\\\s+(?:UNIQUE\\\\s+)?INDEX\\\\s+(?:IF\\\\s+NOT\\\\s+EXISTS\\\\s+)?\"([^\"]+)\"\\\\s+ON\\\\s+(?:\"${schema}\"\\\\.)?\"([^\"]+)\"`,\n    'gi',\n  );\n  for (const match of source.matchAll(rx)) {\n    if (match[1] !== undefined && match[2] !== undefined) owners.set(match[1], match[2]);\n  }\n  return owners;\n}\n\n/**\n * Enum type names from their own definitions rather than from a naming\n * convention — farm's `equipment_category` is an enum and carries no `_enum`\n * suffix. Enums are DELIBERATELY left qualified: existing tenants SHARE the\n * source-schema type (`CREATE TABLE … LIKE INCLUDING ALL` shares a type, it does\n * not clone it), so de-qualifying would split the fleet in two.\n */\nfunction enumTypes(source: string, schema: string): Set<string> {\n  const names = new Set<string>();\n  const rx = new RegExp(`CREATE\\\\s+TYPE\\\\s+\"${schema}\"\\\\.\"([^\"]+)\"\\\\s+AS\\\\s+ENUM`, 'gi');\n  for (const match of source.matchAll(rx)) {\n    if (match[1] !== undefined) names.add(match[1]);\n  }\n  return names;\n}\n\ntype Verdict = 'per-tenant' | 'cross-tenant' | 'enum' | 'retired' | 'unclassified';\n\nfunction classify(\n  identifier: string,\n  registry: Registry,\n  indexes: Map<string, string>,\n  enums: Set<string>,\n): Verdict {\n  if (enums.has(identifier)) return 'enum';\n  if (registry.perTenant.has(identifier)) return 'per-tenant';\n  if (registry.infrastructure.has(identifier)) return 'cross-tenant';\n  if (registry.retired.has(identifier)) return 'retired';\n  const owner = indexes.get(identifier);\n  if (owner !== undefined && owner !== identifier) {\n    return classify(owner, registry, indexes, enums);\n  }\n  return 'unclassified';\n}\n\ninterface Offence {\n  readonly file: string;\n  readonly reason: string;\n}\n\n/** Qualified DDL naming something that must not live in the source schema. */\nfunction qualifiedDdlOffences(file: string, source: string, schema: string): Offence[] {\n  const indexes = indexOwners(source, schema);\n  const enums = enumTypes(source, schema);\n  const registry = registryFor(schema);\n  const offences: Offence[] = [];\n  const seen = new Set<string>();\n\n  const rx = new RegExp(`\"${schema}\"\\\\.\"([^\"]+)\"`, 'g');\n  for (const match of source.matchAll(rx)) {\n    const identifier = match[1];\n    if (identifier === undefined) continue;\n    // Only DDL counts. A qualified name inside a DML statement, a\n    // `to_regclass()` probe or a RAISE message is not a routing decision.\n    if (!DDL_LEAD.test(source.slice(0, match.index))) continue;\n\n    const verdict = classify(identifier, registry, indexes, enums);\n    if (verdict === 'cross-tenant' || verdict === 'enum' || verdict === 'retired') continue;\n    if (seen.has(identifier)) continue;\n    seen.add(identifier);\n\n    offences.push({\n      file,\n      reason:\n        verdict === 'per-tenant'\n          ? `DDL on \"${schema}\".\"${identifier}\" — a PER-TENANT table (MODULE_SCHEMAS.${schema}.tables). ` +\n            `The replay's search_path cannot redirect a qualified name, so every tenant pass writes to ` +\n            `the source schema and the tenant ledger still records it as applied. Drop the \"${schema}\". prefix.`\n          : `DDL on \"${schema}\".\"${identifier}\" — not in MODULE_SCHEMAS.${schema}.tables OR ` +\n            `.infrastructureTables, so this guard cannot tell whether it belongs in a tenant schema. ` +\n            `Register the table, or add the migration to REVIEWED_SOURCE_SCHEMA_DDL with a written reason.`,\n    });\n  }\n  return offences;\n}\n\n/**\n * `pinSearchPath(qr, '<own source schema>')` in a migration that is NOT\n * `@SourceOnlyMigration` — the second spelling of the same defect. The helper\n * (`libs/backend-common/src/database/base-migration.ts`) issues\n * `SET search_path TO \"<its literal argument>\", public` and has no tenant\n * awareness, so it OVERRIDES the runner's tenant pin for the rest of the\n * migration. Legitimate on a source-only migration, where the tenant pass skips\n * the file entirely; a silent tenant-data hole anywhere else.\n */\nfunction sourcePinOffences(file: string, source: string, schema: string): Offence[] {\n  if (/@SourceOnlyMigration\\s*\\(/.test(source)) return [];\n  const rx = new RegExp(\n    `\\\\bpinSearchPath\\\\s*\\\\(\\\\s*[A-Za-z_$][\\\\w$]*\\\\s*,\\\\s*['\"\\`]${schema}['\"\\`]`,\n    'g',\n  );\n  if (!rx.test(source)) return [];\n  return [\n    {\n      file,\n      reason:\n        `pinSearchPath(queryRunner, '${schema}') in a migration that is not @SourceOnlyMigration. ` +\n        `It pins its literal argument, so a tenant pass re-pins the SOURCE schema, the DDL no-ops ` +\n        `against rows that already exist there, and the tenant ledger records a successful apply ` +\n        `anyway (MSG-HIGH-077). Either drop the pin and follow the runner's, or mark the migration ` +\n        `@SourceOnlyMigration if the table really is cross-tenant.`,\n    },\n  ];\n}\n\nfunction migrationFiles(): Array<readonly [string, string]> {\n  const args = ['ls-files', ...TENANT_AWARE_MIGRATION_DIRS.map(([dir]) => `${dir}/[0-9]*.ts`)];\n  const files = execFileSync('git', args, { cwd: REPO_ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' })\n    .split('\\n')\n    .map((line) => line.trim())\n    .filter(Boolean);\n\n  return files.map((file) => {\n    const match = TENANT_AWARE_MIGRATION_DIRS.find(([dir]) => file.startsWith(`${dir}/`));\n    if (match === undefined) throw new Error(`no source schema mapped for ${file}`);\n    return [file, match[1]] as const;\n  });\n}\n\ndescribe('tenant-aware migration DDL guard', () => {\n  it('no tenant-aware migration issues source-schema DDL against a per-tenant table', () => {\n    const offences: Offence[] = [];\n    for (const [file, schema] of migrationFiles()) {\n      if (REVIEWED_SOURCE_SCHEMA_DDL.has(basename(file))) continue;\n      const source = stripComments(readFileSync(join(REPO_ROOT, file), 'utf8'));\n      offences.push(...qualifiedDdlOffences(relative(REPO_ROOT, file), source, schema));\n    }\n\n    expect(offences.map((o) => `${o.file}: ${o.reason}`)).toEqual([]);\n  });\n\n  it('no tenant-aware migration re-pins its own source schema unless it is source-only', () => {\n    const offences: Offence[] = [];\n    for (const [file, schema] of migrationFiles()) {\n      const source = stripComments(readFileSync(join(REPO_ROOT, file), 'utf8'));\n      offences.push(...sourcePinOffences(relative(REPO_ROOT, file), source, schema));\n    }\n\n    expect(offences.map((o) => `${o.file}: ${o.reason}`)).toEqual([]);\n  });\n\n  it('every allowlisted migration still exists (no stale exemptions)', () => {\n    const present = new Set(migrationFiles().map(([f]) => basename(f)));\n    const stale = [...REVIEWED_SOURCE_SCHEMA_DDL].filter((name) => !present.has(name));\n    expect(stale).toEqual([]);\n  });\n\n  it('every retired-table entry names a table the registry really does not carry', () => {\n    const stale: string[] = [];\n    for (const [schema, tables] of RETIRED_SOURCE_TABLES) {\n      const registry = registryFor(schema);\n      for (const table of tables) {\n        if (registry.perTenant.has(table) || registry.infrastructure.has(table)) {\n          stale.push(`${schema}.${table} is registered again — drop it from RETIRED_SOURCE_TABLES`);\n        }\n      }\n    }\n    expect(stale).toEqual([]);\n  });\n});\n","numLines":319,"startLine":1,"totalLines":319}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TdDn4qHx6CB5HzeSSSjjhs","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * The migration set a service ACTUALLY APPLIES — the single answer to a question\n3\t * eight invariants were each answering for themselves, four of them wrongly.\n4\t *\n5\t * # The defect this exists to remove\n6\t *\n7\t * On 2026-05-18 four services squashed their migrations into a `Baseline` and\n8\t * moved the originals to `src/…/migrations/.archive/<timestamp>/`. The archive is\n9\t * dead by construction: every service's TypeORM entry declares\n10\t * `migrations: ['src/…/migrations/[0-9]*…']`, a pattern that neither matches a\n11\t * dot-directory nor descends into one. Fifty-five files stopped running.\n12\t *\n13\t * Invariants that read migrations did not all notice. The idiom\n14\t *\n15\t *     git ls-files 'apps/<svc>/src/migrations/*.ts'\n16\t *\n17\t * looks scoped to one directory and is not: a git pathspec `*` crosses `/`, so\n18\t * it returns the archive too — 32 files for auth-service, 13 of them dead. So\n19\t * `auth-users-tenant-fk.spec.ts` asserted that a FOREIGN KEY exists, PASSED, and\n20\t * was reading an archived migration. The constraint it guards is absent from\n21\t * every migration the runtime applies, and so is `shared.access_logs`, which\n22\t * four other layers still declare and request middleware still writes to.\n23\t * ORPHAN-CRITICAL-516.\n24\t *\n25\t * A gate reporting green off evidence the runtime never executes is worse than\n26\t * no gate: it is a standing claim that something is protected when it is not.\n27\t *\n28\t * # Why a shared module rather than a fix in each spec\n29\t *\n30\t * Four of the eight migration-reading specs already guard against the archive,\n31\t * each in its own way, and `audit-immutability-triggers.spec.ts` even has a\n32\t * private function called `loadMigrationCorpus`. The right pattern was in the\n33\t * repository and half the callers did not use it — the ORPHAN-HIGH-499 shape.\n34\t * Eight private answers to one question is how four of them drift.\n35\t *\n36\t * # Why the directory is DERIVED\n37\t *\n38\t * Services disagree about where migrations live (`src/migrations` for auth,\n39\t * admin, event-store, messaging; `src/database/migrations` for the other ten)\n40\t * and about the glob's suffix spelling. Hardcoding either would put this\n41\t * module's notion of \"effective\" and the runtime's in two places, which is the\n42\t * defect one layer up. `data-source.ts` is the per-service TypeORM entry point\n43\t * CLAUDE.md names as the migration SSoT, and it declares the pattern uniformly,\n44\t * so that is what is read.\n45\t *\n46\t * # What this module does NOT own\n47\t *\n48\t * Whether a service's runtime array actually REGISTERS every file on disk —\n49\t * farm-service uses an explicit array rather than a glob, and\n50\t * `migration-registration-completeness.spec.ts` owns that question. This module\n51\t * answers only \"what is on disk and not retired\", which is the input that spec\n52\t * and the seven others need.\n53\t */\n54\t\n55\timport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';\n56\timport { existsSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\n57\timport { basename, dirname, resolve } from 'node:path';\n58\t\n59\tconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', '..');\n60\t\n61\t/** `migrations: ['src/<something>/[0-9]*…']` in a service's TypeORM entry. */\n62\tconst MIGRATIONS_GLOB = /migrations:\\s*\\[\\s*'(src\\/[A-Za-z0-9_/-]*migrations)\\/\\[0-9\\]\\*/;\n63\t\n64\texport interface MigrationCorpus {\n65\t  /** Service directory name, e.g. `auth-service`. */\n66\t  readonly service: string;\n67\t  /** Repo-relative migrations directory, derived from the service's own entry. */\n68\t  readonly directory: string;\n69\t  /** Repo-relative paths of the migrations the runtime applies, sorted. */\n70\t  readonly files: readonly string[];\n71\t  /** Repo-relative paths of migrations the runtime no longer applies. */\n72\t  readonly archived: readonly string[];\n73\t  /** Concatenated source of the effective set — what a fresh database gets. */\n74\t  readonly source: string;\n75\t}\n76\t\n77\tfunction gitFiles(pathspec: string): string[] {\n78\t  return execFileSync('git', ['-C', REPO_ROOT, 'ls-files', pathspec], { encoding: 'utf8' })\n79\t    .split('\\n')\n80\t    .filter(Boolean);\n81\t}\n82\t\n83\t/**\n84\t * The migrations directory `service` declares, read from its TypeORM entry.\n85\t *\n86\t * Refuses rather than guessing. A service whose entry this cannot parse would\n87\t * otherwise be modelled against the wrong directory — reporting files it does\n88\t * not run, or omitting files it does — which is exactly the error class this\n89\t * module exists to remove.\n90\t */\n91\tfunction migrationsDirectory(service: string): string {\n92\t  const candidates = [\n93\t    `apps/${service}/src/database/data-source.ts`,\n94\t    `apps/${service}/src/data-source.ts`,\n95\t  ].filter((rel) => existsSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, rel)));\n96\t\n97\t  for (const rel of candidates) {\n98\t    const match = MIGRATIONS_GLOB.exec(readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, rel), 'utf8'));\n99\t    if (match?.[1]) return `apps/${service}/${match[1]}`;\n100\t  }\n101\t  throw new Error(\n102\t    `migration-corpus: cannot derive the migrations directory for ${service}. ` +\n103\t      `Looked for a \\`migrations: ['src/…/[0-9]*…']\\` glob in ` +\n104\t      `${candidates.join(', ') || '(no data-source.ts found)'}. This module refuses ` +\n105\t      'to guess: a wrong directory would silently change which migrations count ' +\n106\t      'as effective, which is the defect it exists to prevent.',\n107\t  );\n108\t}\n109\t\n110\t/**\n111\t * True when `rel` is a migration the runtime applies.\n112\t *\n113\t * Mirrors `[0-9]*` exactly: the file sits DIRECTLY in the declared directory and\n114\t * its name begins with a digit. A file under `.archive/` fails the first clause,\n115\t * which is precisely why it is dead — and why a `git ls-files` pathspec, whose\n116\t * `*` crosses `/`, is the wrong instrument for this question.\n117\t */\n118\tfunction isEffective(rel: string, directory: string): boolean {\n119\t  if (dirname(rel) !== directory) return false;\n120\t  const name = basename(rel);\n121\t  return /^[0-9]/.test(name) && (name.endsWith('.ts') || name.endsWith('.js'));\n122\t}\n123\t\n124\tconst cache = new Map<string, MigrationCorpus>();\n125\t\n126\t/** The migrations `service` applies, and the ones it has retired. */\n127\texport function migrationCorpus(service: string): MigrationCorpus {\n128\t  const cached = cache.get(service);\n129\t  if (cached) return cached;\n130\t\n131\t  const directory = migrationsDirectory(service);\n132\t\n133\t  // Deliberately the archive-matching pathspec, so the split is COMPUTED here\n134\t  // rather than assumed. Asking git for only the effective files would make the\n135\t  // `archived` list unknowable, and that list is what proves the split is real.\n136\t  const all = gitFiles(`${directory}/*.ts`);\n137\t  const files = all.filter((rel) => isEffective(rel, directory)).sort();\n138\t  const archived = all.filter((rel) => !isEffective(rel, directory)).sort();\n139\t\n140\t  if (files.length === 0) {\n141\t    throw new Error(\n142\t      `migration-corpus: ${service} has no effective migrations under ${directory}. ` +\n143\t        'Either the service name is wrong or every migration has been archived, ' +\n144\t        'and a spec asserting over an empty corpus would pass vacuously.',\n145\t    );\n146\t  }\n147\t\n148\t  const corpus: MigrationCorpus = {\n149\t    service,\n150\t    directory,\n151\t    files,\n152\t    archived,\n153\t    source: files\n154\t      .map((rel) => `-- ${rel}\\n${readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, rel), 'utf8')}`)\n155\t      .join('\\n'),\n156\t  };\n157\t  cache.set(service, corpus);\n158\t  return corpus;\n159\t}\n160\t\n161\t/** The concatenated effective source of several services, in the order given. */\n162\texport function migrationSource(...services: readonly string[]): string {\n163\t  return services.map((service) => migrationCorpus(service).source).join('\\n---\\n');\n164\t}\n165\t\n166\t/**\n167\t * Every service that owns a migrations directory.\n168\t *\n169\t * `:(glob)` is load-bearing: without the pathspec magic git treats `**` as\n170\t * two ordinary `*`, which cannot match ZERO directories, so services whose\n171\t * migrations live directly under `src/migrations` (admin-api, auth) silently\n172\t * dropped out of the fleet while `src/database/migrations` services stayed in.\n173\t */\n174\texport function servicesWithMigrations(): string[] {\n175\t  const services = new Set<string>();\n176\t  for (const rel of gitFiles(':(glob)apps/*/src/**/migrations/*.ts')) {\n177\t    const service = rel.split('/')[1];\n178\t    if (service) services.add(service);\n179\t  }\n180\t  return [...services].sort();\n181\t}\n182\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b26701de-de54-4da0-aea9-d7d646f071ab","uuid":"2a055f8f-05f3-4855-9530-4a385a981fa9","timestamp":"2026-10-07T10:44:58.921Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-8ce672d7a9e3/tests/invariants/lib/migration-corpus.ts","content":"/**\n * The migration set a service ACTUALLY APPLIES — the single answer to a question\n * eight invariants were each answering for themselves, four of them wrongly.\n *\n * # The defect this exists to remove\n *\n * On 2026-05-18 four services squashed their migrations into a `Baseline` and\n * moved the originals to `src/…/migrations/.archive/<timestamp>/`. The archive is\n * dead by construction: every service's TypeORM entry declares\n * `migrations: ['src/…/migrations/[0-9]*…']`, a pattern that neither matches a\n * dot-directory nor descends into one. Fifty-five files stopped running.\n *\n * Invariants that read migrations did not all notice. The idiom\n *\n *     git ls-files 'apps/<svc>/src/migrations/*.ts'\n *\n * looks scoped to one directory and is not: a git pathspec `*` crosses `/`, so\n * it returns the archive too — 32 files for auth-service, 13 of them dead. So\n * `auth-users-tenant-fk.spec.ts` asserted that a FOREIGN KEY exists, PASSED, and\n * was reading an archived migration. The constraint it guards is absent from\n * every migration the runtime applies, and so is `shared.access_logs`, which\n * four other layers still declare and request middleware still writes to.\n * ORPHAN-CRITICAL-516.\n *\n * A gate reporting green off evidence the runtime never executes is worse than\n * no gate: it is a standing claim that something is protected when it is not.\n *\n * # Why a shared module rather than a fix in each spec\n *\n * Four of the eight migration-reading specs already guard against the archive,\n * each in its own way, and `audit-immutability-triggers.spec.ts` even has a\n * private function called `loadMigrationCorpus`. The right pattern was in the\n * repository and half the callers did not use it — the ORPHAN-HIGH-499 shape.\n * Eight private answers to one question is how four of them drift.\n *\n * # Why the directory is DERIVED\n *\n * Services disagree about where migrations live (`src/migrations` for auth,\n * admin, event-store, messaging; `src/database/migrations` for the other ten)\n * and about the glob's suffix spelling. Hardcoding either would put this\n * module's notion of \"effective\" and the runtime's in two places, which is the\n * defect one layer up. `data-source.ts` is the per-service TypeORM entry point\n * CLAUDE.md names as the migration SSoT, and it declares the pattern uniformly,\n * so that is what is read.\n *\n * # What this module does NOT own\n *\n * Whether a service's runtime array actually REGISTERS every file on disk —\n * farm-service uses an explicit array rather than a glob, and\n * `migration-registration-completeness.spec.ts` owns that question. This module\n * answers only \"what is on disk and not retired\", which is the input that spec\n * and the seven others need.\n */\n\nimport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';\nimport { existsSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\nimport { basename, dirname, resolve } from 'node:path';\n\nconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', '..');\n\n/** `migrations: ['src/<something>/[0-9]*…']` in a service's TypeORM entry. */\nconst MIGRATIONS_GLOB = /migrations:\\s*\\[\\s*'(src\\/[A-Za-z0-9_/-]*migrations)\\/\\[0-9\\]\\*/;\n\nexport interface MigrationCorpus {\n  /** Service directory name, e.g. `auth-service`. */\n  readonly service: string;\n  /** Repo-relative migrations directory, derived from the service's own entry. */\n  readonly directory: string;\n  /** Repo-relative paths of the migrations the runtime applies, sorted. */\n  readonly files: readonly string[];\n  /** Repo-relative paths of migrations the runtime no longer applies. */\n  readonly archived: readonly string[];\n  /** Concatenated source of the effective set — what a fresh database gets. */\n  readonly source: string;\n}\n\nfunction gitFiles(pathspec: string): string[] {\n  return execFileSync('git', ['-C', REPO_ROOT, 'ls-files', pathspec], { encoding: 'utf8' })\n    .split('\\n')\n    .filter(Boolean);\n}\n\n/**\n * The migrations directory `service` declares, read from its TypeORM entry.\n *\n * Refuses rather than guessing. A service whose entry this cannot parse would\n * otherwise be modelled against the wrong directory — reporting files it does\n * not run, or omitting files it does — which is exactly the error class this\n * module exists to remove.\n */\nfunction migrationsDirectory(service: string): string {\n  const candidates = [\n    `apps/${service}/src/database/data-source.ts`,\n    `apps/${service}/src/data-source.ts`,\n  ].filter((rel) => existsSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, rel)));\n\n  for (const rel of candidates) {\n    const match = MIGRATIONS_GLOB.exec(readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, rel), 'utf8'));\n    if (match?.[1]) return `apps/${service}/${match[1]}`;\n  }\n  throw new Error(\n    `migration-corpus: cannot derive the migrations directory for ${service}. ` +\n      `Looked for a \\`migrations: ['src/…/[0-9]*…']\\` glob in ` +\n      `${candidates.join(', ') || '(no data-source.ts found)'}. This module refuses ` +\n      'to guess: a wrong directory would silently change which migrations count ' +\n      'as effective, which is the defect it exists to prevent.',\n  );\n}\n\n/**\n * True when `rel` is a migration the runtime applies.\n *\n * Mirrors `[0-9]*` exactly: the file sits DIRECTLY in the declared directory and\n * its name begins with a digit. A file under `.archive/` fails the first clause,\n * which is precisely why it is dead — and why a `git ls-files` pathspec, whose\n * `*` crosses `/`, is the wrong instrument for this question.\n */\nfunction isEffective(rel: string, directory: string): boolean {\n  if (dirname(rel) !== directory) return false;\n  const name = basename(rel);\n  return /^[0-9]/.test(name) && (name.endsWith('.ts') || name.endsWith('.js'));\n}\n\nconst cache = new Map<string, MigrationCorpus>();\n\n/** The migrations `service` applies, and the ones it has retired. */\nexport function migrationCorpus(service: string): MigrationCorpus {\n  const cached = cache.get(service);\n  if (cached) return cached;\n\n  const directory = migrationsDirectory(service);\n\n  // Deliberately the archive-matching pathspec, so the split is COMPUTED here\n  // rather than assumed. Asking git for only the effective files would make the\n  // `archived` list unknowable, and that list is what proves the split is real.\n  const all = gitFiles(`${directory}/*.ts`);\n  const files = all.filter((rel) => isEffective(rel, directory)).sort();\n  const archived = all.filter((rel) => !isEffective(rel, directory)).sort();\n\n  if (files.length === 0) {\n    throw new Error(\n      `migration-corpus: ${service} has no effective migrations under ${directory}. ` +\n        'Either the service name is wrong or every migration has been archived, ' +\n        'and a spec asserting over an empty corpus would pass vacuously.',\n    );\n  }\n\n  const corpus: MigrationCorpus = {\n    service,\n    directory,\n    files,\n    archived,\n    source: files\n      .map((rel) => `-- ${rel}\\n${readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, rel), 'utf8')}`)\n      .join('\\n'),\n  };\n  cache.set(service, corpus);\n  return corpus;\n}\n\n/** The concatenated effective source of several services, in the order given. */\nexport function migrationSource(...services: readonly string[]): string {\n  return services.map((service) => migrationCorpus(service).source).join('\\n---\\n');\n}\n\n/**\n * Every service that owns a migrations directory.\n *\n * `:(glob)` is load-bearing: without the pathspec magic git treats `**` as\n * two ordinary `*`, which cannot match ZERO directories, so services whose\n * migrations live directly under `src/migrations` (admin-api, auth) silently\n * dropped out of the fleet while `src/database/migrations` services stayed in.\n */\nexport function servicesWithMigrations(): string[] {\n  const services = new Set<string>();\n  for (const rel of gitFiles(':(glob)apps/*/src/**/migrations/*.ts')) {\n    const service = rel.split('/')[1];\n    if (service) services.add(service);\n  }\n  return [...services].sort();\n}\n","numLines":182,"startLine":1,"totalLines":182}}}

After Faz 6, this list should be EMPTY — every\n67\t * baseline migration is forward-only and additive.\n68\t *\n69\t * Each entry is the relative path from REPO_ROOT.\n70\t */\n71\tconst GRANDFATHERED_HISTORICAL_MIGRATIONS = new Set<string>([\n72\t  // 1782200: DROP TABLE shared.audit_logs CASCADE — root incident.\n73\t  'apps/admin-api-service/src/migrations/1782200000000-MoveSharedTablesFromAdminToShared.ts',\n74\t  // 1787200: DROP TABLE shared.audit_logs CASCADE — rebuild after schema realignment.\n75\t  'apps/admin-api-service/src/migrations/1787200000000-RealignSharedAuditLogsSchema.ts',\n76\t  // 1787400: Restore step that follows 1787200's CASCADE. Reads as destructive but\n77\t  // is the restoration path; explicitly grandfathered.\n78\t  'apps/admin-api-service/src/migrations/1787400000000-RestoreSharedAuditLogsImmutability.ts',\n79\t  // 1786900: AlignCodeSequencesSchema — tenant_* fan-out + rename. Reads as\n80\t  // destructive across tenant schemas but is the curated migration.\n81\t  'apps/farm-service/src/database/migrations/1786900000000-AlignCodeSequencesSchema.ts',\n82\t]);\n83\t\n84\t/**\n85\t * Patterns that signify a destructive DDL operation.\n86\t * Each regex captures (when possible) the target table or schema name.\n87\t *\n88\t * Ordered most-blast-radius → least.\n89\t */\n90\tconst DESTRUCTIVE_PATTERNS: Array<{\n91\t  name: string;\n92\t  re: RegExp;\n93\t  targetType: 'schema' | 'table';\n94\t}> = [\n95\t  {\n96\t    name: 'DROP SCHEMA … CASCADE',\n97\t    re: /DROP\\s+SCHEMA\\s+(?:IF\\s+EXISTS\\s+)?[\"']?([a-z_][a-z0-9_]*)[\"']?\\s+CASCADE/gi,\n98\t    targetType: 'schema',\n99\t  },\n100\t  {\n101\t    name: 'DROP TABLE … CASCADE',\n102\t    re: /DROP\\s+TABLE\\s+(?:IF\\s+EXISTS\\s+)?[\"']?([a-z_][a-z0-9_]*\\.[a-z_][a-z0-9_]*)[\"']?\\s+CASCADE/gi,\n103\t    targetType: 'table',\n104\t  },\n105\t  {\n106\t    name: 'DROP TABLE',\n107\t    re: /DROP\\s+TABLE\\s+(?:IF\\s+EXISTS\\s+)?[\"']?([a-z_][a-z0-9_]*\\.[a-z_][a-z0-9_]*)[\"']?(?!\\s*CASCADE)/gi,\n108\t    targetType: 'table',\n109\t  },\n110\t  {\n111\t    name: 'TRUNCATE',\n112\t    re: /TRUNCATE\\s+(?:TABLE\\s+)?(?:ONLY\\s+)?[\"']?([a-z_][a-z0-9_]*\\.[a-z_][a-z0-9_]*)[\"']?/gi,\n113\t    targetType: 'table',\n114\t  },\n115\t];\n116\t\n117\tinterface Violation {\n118\t  file: string;\n119\t  pattern: string;\n120\t  target: string;\n121\t  excerpt: string;\n122\t}\n123\t\n124\t/**\n125\t * Find the start byte of `async down(` in a migration source. Returns\n126\t * `src.length` (no body match) if the migration carries no down() method\n127\t * — in that case the whole file body counts as up()/forward content.\n128\t *\n129\t * Why this matters: a TypeORM migration's down() is the legitimate\n130\t * rollback path. `DROP TABLE foo` inside down() is the inverse of the\n131\t * up() `CREATE TABLE foo` and is structurally correct. The compliance\n132\t * concern is forward-direction destructive operations (up() body) on\n133\t * compliance-critical tables, not their rollback counterparts.\n134\t *\n135\t * Match heuristic: regex catches `async down(`, `public async down(`,\n136\t * `down(queryRunner` etc. — anything that starts a TypeORM down() method.\n137\t */\n138\tfunction findDownMethodStart(src: string): number {\n139\t  const re = /(^|\\n)\\s*(public\\s+|private\\s+|protected\\s+)?async\\s+down\\s*\\(/;\n140\t  const m = re.exec(src);\n141\t  return m ? m.index : src.length;\n142\t}\n143\t\n144\tfunction listMigrationFiles(): string[] {\n145\t  let grepOut: string;\n146\t  try {\n147\t    grepOut = execSync(\n148\t      `git -C ${REPO_ROOT} ls-files 'apps/*/src/migrations/*.ts' 'apps/*/src/migrations/**/*.ts' 'apps/*/src/database/migrations/*.ts' 'apps/*/src/database/migrations/**/*.ts'`,\n149\t      { encoding: 'utf8' },\n150\t    );\n151\t  } catch (err) {\n152\t    const e = err as { status?: number };\n153\t    if (e.status === 1) return [];\n154\t    throw err;\n155\t  }\n156\t  return grepOut\n157\t    .split('\\n')\n158\t    .filter(Boolean)\n159\t    .filter((p) => !p.includes('__tests__'))\n160\t    .filter((p) => !p.endsWith('.spec.ts'))\n161\t    .filter((p) => !p.endsWith('.test.ts'))\n162\t    // ADR-030 day-one reset archived the pre-baseline migration chain into\n163\t    // <migrations>/.archive/<timestamp>/. Those files exist in git history\n164\t    // for forensic reference but are never executed — destructive DDL inside\n165\t    // them is not a production hazard. Treat the archive as out-of-scope.\n166\t    .filter((p) => !p.includes('/.archive/'));\n167\t}\n168\t\n169\tfunction lineContext(src: string, matchIndex: number): string {\n170\t  const start = src.lastIndexOf('\\n', matchIndex) + 1;\n171\t  const end = src.indexOf('\\n', matchIndex);\n172\t  return src.slice(start, end === -1 ? undefined : end).trim();\n173\t}\n174\t\n175\t/**\n176\t * Heuristic: a line that starts with a comment marker (`*`, `//`, `/*`) is\n177\t * a docblock or inline comment, NOT executable SQL. Skip such matches.\n178\t *\n179\t * This avoids false positives like the sensor-service outbox migration\n180\t * whose docblock prose includes the literal text `DROP TABLE sensor.event_outbox CASCADE`\n181\t * as documentation of what NOT to do.\n182\t */\n183\tfunction isCommentLine(line: string): boolean {\n184\t  const trimmed = line.trim();\n185\t  return (\n186\t    trimmed.startsWith('*') ||\n187\t    trimmed.startsWith('//') ||\n188\t    trimmed.startsWith('/*')\n189\t  );\n190\t}\n191\t\n192\tfunction isProtectedTarget(\n193\t  targetType: 'schema' | 'table',\n194\t  target: string,\n195\t): boolean {\n196\t  const lowered = target.toLowerCase();\n197\t  if (targetType === 'schema') {\n198\t    return (PROTECTED_SCHEMAS as readonly string[]).includes(lowered);\n199\t  }\n200\t  // table\n201\t  if ((PROTECTED_TABLES as readonly string[]).includes(lowered)) {\n202\t    return true;\n203\t  }\n204\t  return PROTECTED_TABLE_PATTERNS.some((p) => p.test(lowered));"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b26701de-de54-4da0-aea9-d7d646f071ab","uuid":"9c48c4d7-464f-4a11-a2cd-bf52d480270e","timestamp":"2026-10-07T10:45:11.380Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-8ce672d7a9e3/tests/invariants/protected-tables-guard.spec.ts","content":"import {\n  COMPLIANCE_WAIVER_MARKER_RE,\n  PROTECTED_SCHEMAS,\n  PROTECTED_TABLES,\n  PROTECTED_TABLE_PATTERNS,\n} from '../../libs/backend-common/src/constants/protected-tables';\n\nconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');\n\n/**\n * Pre-day-one-reset historical migrations that ran destructive ops on\n * protected tables. After Faz 6, this list should be EMPTY — every\n * baseline migration is forward-only and additive.\n *\n * Each entry is the relative path from REPO_ROOT.\n */\nconst GRANDFATHERED_HISTORICAL_MIGRATIONS = new Set<string>([\n  // 1782200: DROP TABLE shared.audit_logs CASCADE — root incident.\n  'apps/admin-api-service/src/migrations/1782200000000-MoveSharedTablesFromAdminToShared.ts',\n  // 1787200: DROP TABLE shared.audit_logs CASCADE — rebuild after schema realignment.\n  'apps/admin-api-service/src/migrations/1787200000000-RealignSharedAuditLogsSchema.ts',\n  // 1787400: Restore step that follows 1787200's CASCADE. Reads as destructive but\n  // is the restoration path; explicitly grandfathered.\n  'apps/admin-api-service/src/migrations/1787400000000-RestoreSharedAuditLogsImmutability.ts',\n  // 1786900: AlignCodeSequencesSchema — tenant_* fan-out + rename. Reads as\n  // destructive across tenant schemas but is the curated migration.\n  'apps/farm-service/src/database/migrations/1786900000000-AlignCodeSequencesSchema.ts',\n]);\n\n/**\n * Patterns that signify a destructive DDL operation.\n * Each regex captures (when possible) the target table or schema name.\n *\n * Ordered most-blast-radius → least.\n */\nconst DESTRUCTIVE_PATTERNS: Array<{\n  name: string;\n  re: RegExp;\n  targetType: 'schema' | 'table';\n}> = [\n  {\n    name: 'DROP SCHEMA … CASCADE',\n    re: /DROP\\s+SCHEMA\\s+(?:IF\\s+EXISTS\\s+)?[\"']?([a-z_][a-z0-9_]*)[\"']?\\s+CASCADE/gi,\n    targetType: 'schema',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'DROP TABLE … CASCADE',\n    re: /DROP\\s+TABLE\\s+(?:IF\\s+EXISTS\\s+)?[\"']?([a-z_][a-z0-9_]*\\.[a-z_][a-z0-9_]*)[\"']?\\s+CASCADE/gi,\n    targetType: 'table',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'DROP TABLE',\n    re: /DROP\\s+TABLE\\s+(?:IF\\s+EXISTS\\s+)?[\"']?([a-z_][a-z0-9_]*\\.[a-z_][a-z0-9_]*)[\"']?(?!\\s*CASCADE)/gi,\n    targetType: 'table',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'TRUNCATE',\n    re: /TRUNCATE\\s+(?:TABLE\\s+)?(?:ONLY\\s+)?[\"']?([a-z_][a-z0-9_]*\\.[a-z_][a-z0-9_]*)[\"']?/gi,\n    targetType: 'table',\n  },\n];\n\ninterface Violation {\n  file: string;\n  pattern: string;\n  target: string;\n  excerpt: string;\n}\n\n/**\n * Find the start byte of `async down(` in a migration source. Returns\n * `src.length` (no body match) if the migration carries no down() method\n * — in that case the whole file body counts as up()/forward content.\n *\n * Why this matters: a TypeORM migration's down() is the legitimate\n * rollback path. `DROP TABLE foo` inside down() is the inverse of the\n * up() `CREATE TABLE foo` and is structurally correct. The compliance\n * concern is forward-direction destructive operations (up() body) on\n * compliance-critical tables, not their rollback counterparts.\n *\n * Match heuristic: regex catches `async down(`, `public async down(`,\n * `down(queryRunner` etc. — anything that starts a TypeORM down() method.\n */\nfunction findDownMethodStart(src: string): number {\n  const re = /(^|\\n)\\s*(public\\s+|private\\s+|protected\\s+)?async\\s+down\\s*\\(/;\n  const m = re.exec(src);\n  return m ? m.index : src.length;\n}\n\nfunction listMigrationFiles(): string[] {\n  let grepOut: string;\n  try {\n    grepOut = execSync(\n      `git -C ${REPO_ROOT} ls-files 'apps/*/src/migrations/*.ts' 'apps/*/src/migrations/**/*.ts' 'apps/*/src/database/migrations/*.ts' 'apps/*/src/database/migrations/**/*.ts'`,\n      { encoding: 'utf8' },\n    );\n  } catch (err) {\n    const e = err as { status?: number };\n    if (e.status === 1) return [];\n    throw err;\n  }\n  return grepOut\n    .split('\\n')\n    .filter(Boolean)\n    .filter((p) => !p.includes('__tests__'))\n    .filter((p) => !p.endsWith('.spec.ts'))\n    .filter((p) => !p.endsWith('.test.ts'))\n    // ADR-030 day-one reset archived the pre-baseline migration chain into\n    // <migrations>/.archive/<timestamp>/. Those files exist in git history\n    // for forensic reference but are never executed — destructive DDL inside\n    // them is not a production hazard. Treat the archive as out-of-scope.\n    .filter((p) => !p.includes('/.archive/'));\n}\n\nfunction lineContext(src: string, matchIndex: number): string {\n  const start = src.lastIndexOf('\\n', matchIndex) + 1;\n  const end = src.indexOf('\\n', matchIndex);\n  return src.slice(start, end === -1 ? undefined : end).trim();\n}\n\n/**\n * Heuristic: a line that starts with a comment marker (`*`, `//`, `/*`) is\n * a docblock or inline comment, NOT executable SQL. Skip such matches.\n *\n * This avoids false positives like the sensor-service outbox migration\n * whose docblock prose includes the literal text `DROP TABLE sensor.event_outbox CASCADE`\n * as documentation of what NOT to do.\n */\nfunction isCommentLine(line: string): boolean {\n  const trimmed = line.trim();\n  return (\n    trimmed.startsWith('*') ||\n    trimmed.startsWith('//') ||\n    trimmed.startsWith('/*')\n  );\n}\n\nfunction isProtectedTarget(\n  targetType: 'schema' | 'table',\n  target: string,\n): boolean {\n  const lowered = target.toLowerCase();\n  if (targetType === 'schema') {\n    return (PROTECTED_SCHEMAS as readonly string[]).includes(lowered);\n  }\n  // table\n  if ((PROTECTED_TABLES as readonly string[]).includes(lowered)) {\n    return true;\n  }\n  return PROTECTED_TABLE_PATTERNS.some((p) => p.test(lowered));","numLines":150,"startLine":55,"totalLines":301}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PNFwXnav6uy6NKJvXh2b75","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * Platform-wide invariant — MIGRATION-SPEC-QUARANTINE:\n3\t *\n4\t * `.spec.ts` files MUST NOT live directly inside any service's\n5\t * top-level migration directory. Test specs belong in a sibling\n6\t * `__tests__/` subdirectory — that is where the codebase-wide\n7\t * convention puts every other test, and where\n8\t * `apps/db-migrate/tsconfig.build.json` already excludes them.\n9\t *\n10\t * # Why this invariant exists\n11\t *\n12\t * `apps/db-migrate` is a single CLI that compiles every service's\n13\t * migration source tree into one bundle (see schema-registry.ts).\n14\t * Its `tsconfig.build.json` `include` block pulls each service's\n15\t * `migrations/` directory across `..` boundaries, so a bare\n16\t * `**\\/*.spec.ts` exclude pattern (anchored to db-migrate's tsconfig\n17\t * directory) does NOT reach the cross-tree spec files. The build\n18\t * scope discovers a spec, the spec uses Jest globals, and the\n19\t * production tsconfig deliberately omits `@types/jest` from `types`\n20\t * so that production bundles do not advertise Jest globals — the\n21\t * compile then fails with TS2593 / TS2304.\n22\t *\n23\t * The first instances of this regression were\n24\t * `apps/auth-service/src/migrations/*.spec.ts` and\n25\t * `apps/farm-service/src/database/migrations/*.spec.ts` (commit\n26\t * efddd723), which broke `build` and `Frontend Lighthouse CI` jobs\n27\t * on `main`. The fix moved the specs into `__tests__/` and added\n28\t * cross-tree exclude entries to `tsconfig.build.json` as a Tier-3\n29\t * safety net.\n30\t *\n31\t * This invariant locks in the Tier-1 shape of the fix: any future\n32\t * `*.spec.ts` placed directly inside a top-level `migrations/`\n33\t * directory of a service is a regression and is rejected at PR\n34\t * time, before it reaches the build scope.\n35\t *\n36\t * # Detection contract\n37\t *\n38\t * Walk every service under `apps/<svc>/src/`. For any directory whose\n39\t * leaf segment is `migrations` (i.e. `apps/<svc>/src/migrations/` or\n40\t * `apps/<svc>/src/database/migrations/`), assert that no immediate\n41\t * `.spec.ts` child file exists. Spec files inside a nested\n42\t * `__tests__/` are accepted — they are the canonical placement.\n43\t *\n44\t * # What this invariant does NOT check\n45\t *\n46\t *   - It does not enforce that test specs exist for every migration —\n47\t *     test coverage is a separate concern.\n48\t *   - It does not police `.test.ts` files (the same rule applies in\n49\t *     spirit; the regression only ever surfaced for `.spec.ts` and\n50\t *     `tsconfig.build.json` already covers `**\\/*.test.ts` per\n51\t *     migration tree as a belt-and-braces measure).\n52\t *   - It does not validate that `tsconfig.build.json`'s exclude\n53\t *     entries are paired with each include entry — that pairing is\n54\t *     enforced by the build itself: any future regression compiles\n55\t *     the spec into the bundle and trips TS2593, which `tsc -p\n56\t *     ...tsconfig.build.json` surfaces as a hard failure.\n57\t *\n58\t * # Closure\n59\t *\n60\t * Closes: orphan finding tracked by the build-failure plan at\n61\t * /tmp/ci-cleanup-plans/build-db-migrate.md §6 (\"Long-term invariant\").\n62\t */\n63\t\n64\timport { readdirSync, statSync } from 'node:fs';\n65\timport path from 'node:path';\n66\t\n67\tconst REPO_ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');\n68\tconst APPS_DIR = path.join(REPO_ROOT, 'apps');\n69\t\n70\t/**\n71\t * Recursively walk a directory and yield absolute paths whose leaf\n72\t * directory name is `migrations`. Stops descending into hidden or\n73\t * `node_modules` directories.\n74\t */\n75\tfunction findMigrationDirs(root: string): string[] {\n76\t  const out: string[] = [];\n77\t  const stack: string[] = [root];\n78\t  while (stack.length > 0) {\n79\t    const dir = stack.pop();\n80\t    if (!dir) continue;\n81\t    let entries: string[];\n82\t    try {\n83\t      entries = readdirSync(dir);\n84\t    } catch {\n85\t      continue;\n86\t    }\n87\t    for (const entry of entries) {\n88\t      if (entry.startsWith('.') || entry === 'node_modules') continue;\n89\t      const full = path.join(dir, entry);\n90\t      let st: ReturnType<typeof statSync>;\n91\t      try {\n92\t        st = statSync(full);\n93\t      } catch {\n94\t        continue;\n95\t      }\n96\t      if (!st.isDirectory()) continue;\n97\t      if (entry === 'migrations') {\n98\t        out.push(full);\n99\t        // Do NOT descend into `migrations/` itself for further\n100\t        // discovery — nested `migrations/` directories are not a\n101\t        // pattern in this codebase, and descending would re-flag\n102\t        // the same directory on its child path.\n103\t        continue;\n104\t      }\n105\t      stack.push(full);\n106\t    }\n107\t  }\n108\t  return out;\n109\t}\n110\t\n111\t/**\n112\t * List immediate `.spec.ts` children of a migrations/ directory.\n113\t * Files inside a nested `__tests__/` are NOT immediate children\n114\t * and are not returned.\n115\t */\n116\tfunction listTopLevelSpecs(migrationsDir: string): string[] {\n117\t  let entries: string[];\n118\t  try {\n119\t    entries = readdirSync(migrationsDir);\n120\t  } catch {\n121\t    return [];\n122\t  }\n123\t  return entries\n124\t    .filter((e) => e.endsWith('.spec.ts'))\n125\t    .filter((e) => {\n126\t      const full = path.join(migrationsDir, e);\n127\t      try {\n128\t        return statSync(full).isFile();\n129\t      } catch {\n130\t        return false;\n131\t      }\n132\t    })\n133\t    .map((e) => path.join(migrationsDir, e));\n134\t}\n135\t\n136\tdescribe('INVARIANT: migration spec quarantine', () => {\n137\t  it('asserts no .spec.ts file lives directly inside any service migrations/ directory', () => {\n138\t    let services: string[];\n139\t    try {\n140\t      services = readdirSync(APPS_DIR);\n141\t    } catch (err) {\n142\t      throw new Error(\n143\t        `apps/ directory not readable at ${APPS_DIR}: ${(err as Error).message}`,\n144\t      );\n145\t    }\n146\t\n147\t    const violations: string[] = [];\n148\t    for (const svc of services) {\n149\t      const svcSrc = path.join(APPS_DIR, svc, 'src');\n150\t      let st: ReturnType<typeof statSync>;\n151\t      try {\n152\t        st = statSync(svcSrc);\n153\t      } catch {\n154\t        continue; // service has no src/ — not applicable\n155\t      }\n156\t      if (!st.isDirectory()) continue;\n157\t\n158\t      const migrationDirs = findMigrationDirs(svcSrc);\n159\t      for (const dir of migrationDirs) {\n160\t        const specs = listTopLevelSpecs(dir);\n161\t        for (const spec of specs) {\n162\t          violations.push(path.relative(REPO_ROOT, spec));\n163\t        }\n164\t      }\n165\t    }\n166\t\n167\t    if (violations.length > 0) {\n168\t      const lines = violations.map((v) => `  - ${v}`).join('\\n');\n169\t      throw new Error(\n170\t        [\n171\t          'Found .spec.ts file(s) placed directly inside a service migrations/ directory.',\n172\t          'These break `db-migrate:build:production` because the db-migrate tsconfig',\n173\t          'pulls every service migrations/ tree across `..` boundaries while keeping',\n174\t          '`@types/jest` deliberately out of `types` (production bundles must not',\n175\t          'advertise Jest globals). Move each spec into a sibling `__tests__/`',\n176\t          'subdirectory:',\n177\t          '',\n178\t          lines,\n179\t          '',\n180\t          'Example:',\n181\t          '  apps/<svc>/src/migrations/2026-foo.spec.ts',\n182\t          '   -> apps/<svc>/src/migrations/__tests__/2026-foo.spec.ts',\n183\t          '',\n184\t          'See /tmp/ci-cleanup-plans/build-db-migrate.md for the full rationale,',\n185\t          'and `apps/db-migrate/tsconfig.build.json` for the matching exclude',\n186\t          'entries that act as the Tier-3 safety net.',\n187\t        ].join('\\n'),\n188\t      );\n189\t    }\n190\t  });\n191\t});\n192\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b26701de-de54-4da0-aea9-d7d646f071ab","uuid":"9387af42-7453-47f2-8951-e22f9f10f73b","timestamp":"2026-10-07T10:45:48.744Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-8ce672d7a9e3/tests/invariants/migration-spec-quarantine.spec.ts","content":"/**\n * Platform-wide invariant — MIGRATION-SPEC-QUARANTINE:\n *\n * `.spec.ts` files MUST NOT live directly inside any service's\n * top-level migration directory. Test specs belong in a sibling\n * `__tests__/` subdirectory — that is where the codebase-wide\n * convention puts every other test, and where\n * `apps/db-migrate/tsconfig.build.json` already excludes them.\n *\n * # Why this invariant exists\n *\n * `apps/db-migrate` is a single CLI that compiles every service's\n * migration source tree into one bundle (see schema-registry.ts).\n * Its `tsconfig.build.json` `include` block pulls each service's\n * `migrations/` directory across `..` boundaries, so a bare\n * `**\\/*.spec.ts` exclude pattern (anchored to db-migrate's tsconfig\n * directory) does NOT reach the cross-tree spec files. The build\n * scope discovers a spec, the spec uses Jest globals, and the\n * production tsconfig deliberately omits `@types/jest` from `types`\n * so that production bundles do not advertise Jest globals — the\n * compile then fails with TS2593 / TS2304.\n *\n * The first instances of this regression were\n * `apps/auth-service/src/migrations/*.spec.ts` and\n * `apps/farm-service/src/database/migrations/*.spec.ts` (commit\n * efddd723), which broke `build` and `Frontend Lighthouse CI` jobs\n * on `main`. The fix moved the specs into `__tests__/` and added\n * cross-tree exclude entries to `tsconfig.build.json` as a Tier-3\n * safety net.\n *\n * This invariant locks in the Tier-1 shape of the fix: any future\n * `*.spec.ts` placed directly inside a top-level `migrations/`\n * directory of a service is a regression and is rejected at PR\n * time, before it reaches the build scope.\n *\n * # Detection contract\n *\n * Walk every service under `apps/<svc>/src/`. For any directory whose\n * leaf segment is `migrations` (i.e. `apps/<svc>/src/migrations/` or\n * `apps/<svc>/src/database/migrations/`), assert that no immediate\n * `.spec.ts` child file exists. Spec files inside a nested\n * `__tests__/` are accepted — they are the canonical placement.\n *\n * # What this invariant does NOT check\n *\n *   - It does not enforce that test specs exist for every migration —\n *     test coverage is a separate concern.\n *   - It does not police `.test.ts` files (the same rule applies in\n *     spirit; the regression only ever surfaced for `.spec.ts` and\n *     `tsconfig.build.json` already covers `**\\/*.test.ts` per\n *     migration tree as a belt-and-braces measure).\n *   - It does not validate that `tsconfig.build.json`'s exclude\n *     entries are paired with each include entry — that pairing is\n *     enforced by the build itself: any future regression compiles\n *     the spec into the bundle and trips TS2593, which `tsc -p\n *     ...tsconfig.build.json` surfaces as a hard failure.\n *\n * # Closure\n *\n * Closes: orphan finding tracked by the build-failure plan at\n * /tmp/ci-cleanup-plans/build-db-migrate.md §6 (\"Long-term invariant\").\n */\n\nimport { readdirSync, statSync } from 'node:fs';\nimport path from 'node:path';\n\nconst REPO_ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');\nconst APPS_DIR = path.join(REPO_ROOT, 'apps');\n\n/**\n * Recursively walk a directory and yield absolute paths whose leaf\n * directory name is `migrations`. Stops descending into hidden or\n * `node_modules` directories.\n */\nfunction findMigrationDirs(root: string): string[] {\n  const out: string[] = [];\n  const stack: string[] = [root];\n  while (stack.length > 0) {\n    const dir = stack.pop();\n    if (!dir) continue;\n    let entries: string[];\n    try {\n      entries = readdirSync(dir);\n    } catch {\n      continue;\n    }\n    for (const entry of entries) {\n      if (entry.startsWith('.') || entry === 'node_modules') continue;\n      const full = path.join(dir, entry);\n      let st: ReturnType<typeof statSync>;\n      try {\n        st = statSync(full);\n      } catch {\n        continue;\n      }\n      if (!st.isDirectory()) continue;\n      if (entry === 'migrations') {\n        out.push(full);\n        // Do NOT descend into `migrations/` itself for further\n        // discovery — nested `migrations/` directories are not a\n        // pattern in this codebase, and descending would re-flag\n        // the same directory on its child path.\n        continue;\n      }\n      stack.push(full);\n    }\n  }\n  return out;\n}\n\n/**\n * List immediate `.spec.ts` children of a migrations/ directory.\n * Files inside a nested `__tests__/` are NOT immediate children\n * and are not returned.\n */\nfunction listTopLevelSpecs(migrationsDir: string): string[] {\n  let entries: string[];\n  try {\n    entries = readdirSync(migrationsDir);\n  } catch {\n    return [];\n  }\n  return entries\n    .filter((e) => e.endsWith('.spec.ts'))\n    .filter((e) => {\n      const full = path.join(migrationsDir, e);\n      try {\n        return statSync(full).isFile();\n      } catch {\n        return false;\n      }\n    })\n    .map((e) => path.join(migrationsDir, e));\n}\n\ndescribe('INVARIANT: migration spec quarantine', () => {\n  it('asserts no .spec.ts file lives directly inside any service migrations/ directory', () => {\n    let services: string[];\n    try {\n      services = readdirSync(APPS_DIR);\n    } catch (err) {\n      throw new Error(\n        `apps/ directory not readable at ${APPS_DIR}: ${(err as Error).message}`,\n      );\n    }\n\n    const violations: string[] = [];\n    for (const svc of services) {\n      const svcSrc = path.join(APPS_DIR, svc, 'src');\n      let st: ReturnType<typeof statSync>;\n      try {\n        st = statSync(svcSrc);\n      } catch {\n        continue; // service has no src/ — not applicable\n      }\n      if (!st.isDirectory()) continue;\n\n      const migrationDirs = findMigrationDirs(svcSrc);\n      for (const dir of migrationDirs) {\n        const specs = listTopLevelSpecs(dir);\n        for (const spec of specs) {\n          violations.push(path.relative(REPO_ROOT, spec));\n        }\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (violations.length > 0) {\n      const lines = violations.map((v) => `  - ${v}`).join('\\n');\n      throw new Error(\n        [\n          'Found .spec.ts file(s) placed directly inside a service migrations/ directory.',\n          'These break `db-migrate:build:production` because the db-migrate tsconfig',\n          'pulls every service migrations/ tree across `..` boundaries while keeping',\n          '`@types/jest` deliberately out of `types` (production bundles must not',\n          'advertise Jest globals). Move each spec into a sibling `__tests__/`',\n          'subdirectory:',\n          '',\n          lines,\n          '',\n          'Example:',\n          '  apps/<svc>/src/migrations/2026-foo.spec.ts',\n          '   -> apps/<svc>/src/migrations/__tests__/2026-foo.spec.ts',\n          '',\n          'See /tmp/ci-cleanup-plans/build-db-migrate.md for the full rationale,',\n          'and `apps/db-migrate/tsconfig.build.json` for the matching exclude',\n          'entries that act as the Tier-3 safety net.',\n        ].join('\\n'),\n      );\n    }\n  });\n});\n","numLines":192,"startLine":1,"totalLines":192}}}

}\n}\n\n/**\n * Per-service migration manifest.\n *\n * Each entry maps a service to:\n *   - `schema`: the Postgres schema this service owns. The DataSource is\n *     bound to it so the migration ledger lands in `<schema>.migrations`.\n *   - `migrationsDir`: the on-disk path containing the service's\n *     timestamped TypeORM migration files. Two patterns exist in this\n *     codebase (historical drift, ADR-011 §\"Migration Runners\"):\n *       * `apps/<svc>/src/migrations/`         (auth, admin-api, messaging, event-store)\n *       * `apps/<svc>/src/database/migrations/` (everything else)\n *     This manifest reflects the actual on-disk layout — keep in sync\n *     when adding a service.\n *\n * Order matters: services with cross-schema FK migrations (e.g.\n * messaging.tenant_ai_settings -> auth.tenants) must run after the\n * schema they reference. We run auth FIRST for that reason; everything\n * else is alphabetical.\n */\ninterface ServiceManifest {\n  name: string;\n  schema: string;\n  migrationsDir: string;\n  /**\n   * `entitiesGlob`: optional glob (relative to repo root) that matches\n   * every `*.entity.ts` file owned by this service. When set, the\n   * entity-surface-vs-DB matrix (assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb) iterates\n   * the files under this glob and asserts each entity's table surface\n   * exists in the DB. The target is not an ordinary table: the docblock at :12 states it is a PER-TENANT cloned table, and :15 states the operative rule — the runner pins search_path to the messaging source template and then to each tenant_<uuid> schema, so the name MUST stay UNQUALIFIED or the DROP touches only the template and every tenant keeps the table forever. down() recreates the table shape but not its rows (:21), so the forward step is lossy and cannot be undone by replay.\\n\\nWHY COVERAGE MATTERS, CONCRETELY. The unqualified-name property is the entire difference between a drop that reaches every tenant and one that reaches a single schema while the tenant migration ledger still records a successful apply. Nothing executable enforces it — the rule lives in a comment, and a comment constrains no behavior. WHAT BREAKS IF IT IS SKIPPED: the failure is silent rather than loud. The migration reports applied, every tenant schema retains a stale aiEnabled master switch that ai-service no longer reads, and the duplicate-source-of-truth condition this change exists to remove survives in production per-tenant data. A silent partial drop is strictly worse than a failed one, because the ledger then certifies work that never happened.\\n\\nDOWNSTREAM SURFACE AFFECTED: messaging-service's tenant schema set, and the invariants project that depends on messaging-service.\\n\\nTHE ABSENCE HALF, AND THE GAP I AM DISCLOSING. The request supplied exactly one admissible ref — the migration itself — and a single file cannot by construction prove what exists elsewhere in the tree, so I resolved the absence by enumerating the repository within the allowed scope and report that searched scope here rather than citing paths I was not given. messaging-service has no src/migrations/__tests__ directory at all, while thirty per-migration specs exist under precisely that convention across admin-api, auth, config, farm and sensor services, covering comparable destructive and erasure migrations; and no test imports the migration class, whose only importer is the service's own app.module registration. The fleet-wide migration invariants do enumerate this file, but the single guard that matches this hazard class classifies this table through a retired-table exemption and skips it, so the unqualified-name property is checked by no test at this snapshot. That exemption is what converts a heuristic-looking finding into a real gap.\\n\\nRECOMMENDED ROOT-CAUSE FIX, NOT A SEVERITY REDUCTION. Add apps/messaging-service/src/migrations/__tests__/1802100000000-DropTenantAiSettings.spec.ts following the established convention, asserting that the up() SQL carries no schema qualifier and that a tenant-schema replay removes the table in each tenant_<uuid> schema; and remove the messaging/tenant_ai_settings retired-table exemption from the tenant-aware DDL guard so the property is machine-checked instead of described. That moves the guarantee from prose into the build.\\n\\nCONFIDENCE 0.72. The hazard is proven directly from the cited lines and the adjacent/importing absence is verified by enumeration.
